 succeeded in 0ms:
/**
 * break-no-secrets-gate.ts — verify `check-no-secrets` by BREAKING it.
 *
 *   bun run tools/break-no-secrets-gate.ts              # every probe
 *   bun run tools/break-no-secrets-gate.ts --self-test  # verify this harness
 *
 * # Why this exists
 *
 * A gate that has never been broken is a claim. This repository recorded four
 * gates that reported PASS while the thing they checked was broken (`CLAIMS.md`
 * E-165, E-168, E-182, E-201), and each was found by mutating, not by reading.
 * So the mutations are committed and the next session re-runs them.
 *
 * # Three kinds of probe
 *
 * * **Detector mutations.** A copy of the gate with ONE exact edit, written
 *   beside it as `tools/.mutant-<pid>.ts` (gitignored) so its imports resolve,
 *   run with `--controls-only`. It must end `CONTROL-FAILED exit=2`, and the
 *   control the probe names must appear in a `CONTROL FAILED:` line. The real
 *   gate file is never edited, so nothing needs restoring.
 * * **Tree plants.** An untracked file carrying a SYNTHETIC secret is written
 *   into the real tree, and the real gate runs over the real tree. It must end
 *   `FAIL exit=1` with the planted path in the `FAILING` section. ⚠ **A plant
 *   into a gitignored path must still PASS**, because ignored files are not
 *   published. ⚠ **These need a clean baseline**: the unplanted tree must PASS
 *   first, or a FAIL proves nothing about the plant.
 * * **Baselines.** The unmutated copy must end `CONTROLS-PASS exit=0`, so a
 *   harness that rejects everything cannot score full marks.
 *
 * # How a run is classified
 *
 * By the gate's verdict line, which must be the LAST line and must agree with
 * the exit code. Bun exits 1 on a syntax error or an uncaught throw, the same
 * code as a real finding, so the exit code alone cannot tell them apart
 * (`CLAIMS.md` E-259). Anything else is `CRASH`. ⚠ The needle is searched only
 * in the lines that report a failure, never the whole output: the gate prints
 * its control names, so an unscoped search matches every run.
 *
 * ⚠ Tree plants write files into the working tree for a few seconds each. The
 * values are generated here and are not anyone's secret, but do not run
 * `git add -A` while this runs.
 */

import { randomBytes } from "node:crypto";
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, readFileSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { homedir, tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
import { encodeSeed } from "ripple-address-codec";

const ROOT = join(import.meta.dir, "..");
const GATE = join(import.meta.dir, "check-no-secrets.ts");
const SRC = readFileSync(GATE, "utf8");
const MUTANT = join(import.meta.dir, `.mutant-${process.pid}.ts`);

const cleanup: string[] = [MUTANT];
const clean = () => { for (const p of cleanup) rmSync(p, { recursive: true, force: true }); };
process.on("exit", clean);
for (const sig of ["SIGINT", "SIGTERM"] as const) process.on(sig, () => { clean(); process.exit(130); });

interface Run { verdict: string; exit: number; out: string[]; crash: boolean }
function run(file: string, args: string[]): Run {
  const r = Bun.spawnSync(["bun", "run", file, ...args], { cwd: ROOT, stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe" });
  const out = new TextDecoder().decode(r.stdout).split("\n").filter((l) => l.length);
  const last = out[out.length - 1] ?? "";
  const m = last.match(/^check-no-secrets: verdict=(\S+) exit=(\d+)$/);
  const crash = !m || Number(m[2]) !== r.exitCode;
  if (crash) out.push(...new TextDecoder().decode(r.stderr).split("\n").slice(-8).map((l) => `stderr: ${l}`));
  return { verdict: m?.[1] ?? "none", exit: r.exitCode ?? -1, out, crash };
}

/** Lines that report a failure, and only those. */
function failureLines(r: Run): string[] {
  const control = r.out.filter((l) => l.startsWith("  CONTROL FAILED:") || l.startsWith("  STALE CITED VECTOR:"));
  const i = r.out.findIndex((l) => l.startsWith("FAILING:"));
  if (i === -1) return control;
  const end = r.out.findIndex((l, k) => k > i && (l.startsWith("REPORTED") || l.startsWith("What this gate")));
  return [...control, ...r.out.slice(i, end === -1 ? undefined : end)];
}

interface Mutation { name: string; find: string; replace: string; needle: string; args?: string[]; expect?: "CONTROL-FAILED" }

/** Apply one exact edit, asserting it matches exactly once. */
function mutate(m: Mutation): string | null {
  const n = SRC.split(m.find).length - 1;
  return n === 1 ? SRC.replace(m.find, m.replace) : null;
}

export const MUTATIONS: Mutation[] = [
  { name: "near-seeds require a checksum", find: "if (run[0] === \"s\" && run.length <= 35) consider(0, run.length, false);", replace: "if (run[0] === \"s\" && run.length <= 35) consider(0, run.length, true);", needle: "a seed whose checksum fails" },
  { name: "no sliding over glued runs", find: "      if (run[i] !== \"s\") continue;", replace: "      if (run[i] !== \"s\" || true) continue;", needle: "glued to other base58" },
  { name: "public-entropy rule accepts any entropy", find: "matches: (e) => e.length === 16 && e.every((b) => b === e[0]),", replace: "matches: (e) => e.length === 16,", needle: "a secp256k1 seed inside a comment" },
  { name: "'sk' dropped from the secret labels", find: "const SECRET_COMPONENTS = new Set([\"sk\", ", replace: "const SECRET_COMPONENTS = new Set([\"xk\", ", needle: "under a JSON 'sk' key" },
  { name: "label window too short for a type annotation", find: "const WINDOW = 40;", replace: "const WINDOW = 4;", needle: "behind a Rust type annotation" },
  { name: "'entropy' dropped from the entropy labels", find: "new Set([\"seed\", \"entropy\",", replace: "new Set([\"seed\", \"xentropy\",", needle: "entropy in hex" },
  { name: "known-value class disabled", find: "  if (!store.needles.length) return [];", replace: "  if (true) return [];", needle: "unlabelled and lower-cased" },
  { name: "derived ElGamal key not searched", find: "    { value: deriveElGamalKey(seed).sk.toLowerCase(), label: `${label} (ElGamal key derived from it)`, kind: \"hex\" },\n", replace: "", needle: "DERIVED from a stored seed" },
  { name: "JSON-escaped home rule removed", find: "{ rule: \"JSON-escaped home\", re: new RegExp(", replace: "{ rule: \"JSON-escaped home\", re: new RegExp(\"(?!)\", \"g\") ?? new RegExp(", needle: "JSON-escaped home path" },
  { name: "dash-encoded home rule removed", find: "{ rule: \"dash-encoded home\", re: /", replace: "{ rule: \"dash-encoded home\", re: /(?!)/g ?? /", needle: "dash-encoded home path" },
  { name: "Linux home rule removed", find: "{ rule: \"Linux home\", re: new RegExp(", replace: "{ rule: \"Linux home\", re: new RegExp(\"(?!)\", \"g\") ?? new RegExp(", needle: "Linux home path" },
  { name: "Windows home rule removed", find: "{ rule: \"Windows home\", re: new RegExp(", replace: "{ rule: \"Windows home\", re: new RegExp(\"(?!)\", \"g\") ?? new RegExp(", needle: "Windows home path" },
  { name: "URL-encoded home rule removed", find: "{ rule: \"URL-encoded home\", re: new RegExp(", replace: "{ rule: \"URL-encoded home\", re: new RegExp(\"(?!)\", \"g\") ?? new RegExp(", needle: "URL-encoded home path" },
  { name: "per-user temp rule removed", find: "{ rule: \"macOS per-user temp\", re: new RegExp(", replace: "{ rule: \"macOS per-user temp\", re: new RegExp(\"(?!)\", \"g\") ?? new RegExp(", needle: "per-user temp path" },
  { name: "every user name treated as generic", find: "const generic = rule !== \"macOS per-user temp\" && GENERIC_USERS.has(who.toLowerCase());", replace: "const generic = rule !== \"macOS per-user temp\";", needle: "a macOS home path" },
  { name: "'.com' treated as reserved", find: "const RESERVED_TLDS = new Set([\"example\", \"invalid\", \"test\", \"localhost\"]);", replace: "const RESERVED_TLDS = new Set([\"example\", \"invalid\", \"test\", \"localhost\", \"com\"]);", needle: "personal email inside docs/reviews" },
  { name: "email local part never expanded", find: "while (s > 0 && i - s < 64 && isLocalChar(", replace: "while (s > 0 && i - s < 0 && isLocalChar(", needle: "personal email inside docs/reviews" },
  { name: "commit headers scanned", find: "  return msg;\n}", replace: "  return t;\n}", needle: "author's identity" },
  { name: "untracked files not listed", find: "\"--cached\", \"--others\", \"--exclude-standard\"", replace: "\"--cached\", \"--exclude-standard\"", needle: "untracked, unignored" },
  { name: "gitignored files listed", find: "\"--cached\", \"--others\", \"--exclude-standard\"", replace: "\"--cached\", \"--others\"", needle: "gitignored file" },
  { name: "index copy of a deleted file ignored", find: "      try { units.push({ where: n, text: asText(git(repo, [\"cat-file\", \"blob\", `:${n}`])), scope: \"tree\", kind: \"file\" }); } catch { /* not in index either */ }\n", replace: "", needle: "staged and then deleted" },
  // Shaped like a real regression: it checks the target exists before reading it,
  // so it does not crash on a dangling link and trip the wrong control.
  { name: "symlinks followed", find: "if (s.isSymbolicLink()) units.push({ where: `${n} (symlink target)`, text: readlinkSync(p), scope: \"tree\", kind: \"file\" });", replace: "if (s.isSymbolicLink()) { units.push({ where: `${n} (symlink target)`, text: readlinkSync(p), scope: \"tree\", kind: \"file\" }); if (existsSync(p)) units.push({ where: n, text: asText(readFileSync(p)), scope: \"tree\", kind: \"file\" }); }", needle: "does not follow a symlink" },
  { name: "binary files skipped", find: "else if (s.isFile()) units.push(", replace: "else if (s.isFile() && !readFileSync(p).includes(0)) units.push(", needle: "inside a binary file" },
  { name: "commit messages not scanned", find: "else if (o.type === \"commit\" || o.type === \"tag\") units.push(", replace: "else if (false) units.push(", needle: "in a commit message" },
  { name: "history paths not scanned", find: "  for (const p of paths) units.push(", replace: "  for (const p of [] as string[]) units.push(", needle: "tracked state.json by path" },
  { name: "state.json dropped from secret-file names", find: "(^|\\/)(state\\.json|", replace: "(^|\\/)(xstate\\.json|", needle: "a tracked file named state.json" },
  { name: "GitHub token pattern removed", find: "{ name: \"GitHub token\", re: /", replace: "{ name: \"GitHub token\", re: /(?!)/g ?? /", needle: "a GitHub token" },
  { name: "PEM pattern removed", find: "{ name: \"PEM private key\", re: /", replace: "{ name: \"PEM private key\", re: /(?!)/g ?? /", needle: "a PEM private key" },
  { name: "personal data in history fails by default", find: "d.findings.filter((f) => f.scope === \"tree\" || d.strictHistory || !PERSONAL.has(f.cls));", replace: "d.findings.filter(() => true);", needle: "does not fail by default" },
  { name: "an empty store does not refuse", find: "if (d.requireStore && d.store.secrets === 0) return", replace: "if (false && d.requireStore && d.store.secrets === 0) return", needle: "empty store" },
  { name: "base64 decoded and scanned (a LIMIT becomes caught)", find: "...seedHits(text), ...labelledHits(text),", replace: "...seedHits(text), ...seedHits(Buffer.from(text, \"base64\").toString(\"latin1\")), ...labelledHits(text),", needle: "limits/base64.txt" },
  { name: "the store counts duplicates", find: "    if (seen.has(seed)) return;\n", replace: "", needle: "loads exactly two secrets" },
  {
    name: "a cited vector nobody cites is caught as stale",
    find: "export const CITED_VECTORS: { value: string; source: string }[] = [\n",
    replace: "export const CITED_VECTORS: { value: string; source: string }[] = [\n  { value: \"" + "ab".repeat(32) + "\", source: \"a planted stale entry\" },\n",
    needle: "a planted stale entry",
    args: ["--tree-only", "--no-local-store"],
  },
];

interface Plant { name: string; files: Record<string, string>; store?: Record<string, string>; expect: "FAIL" | "PASS"; needle?: string }

function plants(): Plant[] {
  const tag = `zz-break-${process.pid}`;
  const sk = randomBytes(32).toString("hex");
  return [
    { name: "a real-entropy seed in an untracked file", files: { [`${tag}.txt`]: `seed: ${encodeSeed(randomBytes(16), "ed25519")}\n` }, expect: "FAIL", needle: `${tag}.txt` },
    { name: "an ElGamal secret under docs/reviews", files: { [`docs/reviews/${tag}.md`]: `{"sk": "${randomBytes(32).toString("hex")}"}\n` }, expect: "FAIL", needle: `docs/reviews/${tag}.md` },
    { name: "a stored secret, bare, against a synthetic store", files: { [`${tag}-known.txt`]: `${sk}\n` }, store: { "state.json": JSON.stringify({ x: { sk } }) }, expect: "FAIL", needle: `${tag}-known.txt` },
    { name: "this machine's home path in an evidence README", files: { [`docs/evidence/${tag}/README.md`]: `ran from ${homedir()}/x\n` }, expect: "FAIL", needle: `docs/evidence/${tag}/README.md` },
    { name: "a seed in a gitignored file still PASSES", files: { [`${tag}.log`]: `${encodeSeed(randomBytes(16), "secp256k1")}\n` }, expect: "PASS" },
  ];
}

function plantRun(p: Plant): Run {
  const written: string[] = [];
  let storeDir: string | undefined;
  try {
    for (const [rel, body] of Object.entries(p.files)) {
      const abs = join(ROOT, rel);
      if (existsSync(abs)) throw new Error(`refusing to overwrite ${rel}`);
      // Remove the shallowest directory this plant creates, or the file itself.
      let created = abs;
      for (let d = dirname(abs); d !== ROOT && !existsSync(d); d = dirname(d)) created = d;
      mkdirSync(dirname(abs), { recursive: true });
      writeFileSync(abs, body);
      written.push(created);
      cleanup.push(created);
    }
    const args = ["--tree-only"];
    if (p.store) {
      storeDir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "break-no-secrets-store-"));
      cleanup.push(storeDir);
      for (const [n, b] of Object.entries(p.store)) writeFileSync(join(storeDir, n), b);
      args.push("--store", storeDir);
    }
    return run(GATE, args);
  } finally {
    for (const w of written) rmSync(w, { recursive: true, force: true });
    if (storeDir) rmSync(storeDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
  }
}

type Verdict = "TICK" | "NOT-CAUGHT" | "WRONG-CONTROL" | "CRASH" | "DID-NOT-APPLY";

function judgeMutation(m: Mutation): { v: Verdict; detail: string } {
  const body = mutate(m);
  if (body === null) return { v: "DID-NOT-APPLY", detail: `the edit matched ${SRC.split(m.find).length - 1} times, not once` };
  writeFileSync(MUTANT, body);
  const r = run(MUTANT, m.args ?? ["--controls-only"]);
  if (r.crash) return { v: "CRASH", detail: r.out.slice(-4).join(" | ") };
  if (r.verdict !== (m.expect ?? "CONTROL-FAILED") || r.exit !== 2) return { v: "NOT-CAUGHT", detail: `verdict=${r.verdict} exit=${r.exit}` };
  const lines = failureLines(r);
  if (!lines.some((l) => l.includes(m.needle))) return { v: "WRONG-CONTROL", detail: `failed, but not on '${m.needle}': ${lines.slice(0, 3).join(" | ")}` };
  return { v: "TICK", detail: lines.filter((l) => l.includes(m.needle))[0].trim() };
}

function judgePlant(p: Plant): { v: Verdict; detail: string } {
  const r = plantRun(p);
  if (r.crash) return { v: "CRASH", detail: r.out.slice(-4).join(" | ") };
  if (p.expect === "PASS") return r.exit === 0 && r.verdict === "PASS" ? { v: "TICK", detail: "PASS" } : { v: "NOT-CAUGHT", detail: `expected PASS, got verdict=${r.verdict} exit=${r.exit}` };
  if (r.verdict !== "FAIL" || r.exit !== 1) return { v: "NOT-CAUGHT", detail: `verdict=${r.verdict} exit=${r.exit}` };
  const lines = failureLines(r);
  if (!lines.some((l) => l.includes(p.needle!))) return { v: "WRONG-CONTROL", detail: `failed, but not naming '${p.needle}'` };
  return { v: "TICK", detail: lines.filter((l) => l.includes(p.needle!))[0].trim() };
}

function finish(ok: boolean, n: number, bad: number): never {
  console.log(`break-no-secrets-gate: verdict=${ok ? "PASS" : "FAIL"} probes=${n} failed=${bad}`);
  process.exit(ok ? 0 : 1);
}

if (import.meta.main) {
  const selfTest = process.argv.includes("--self-test");
  let n = 0, bad = 0;
  const report = (name: string, want: Verdict | "REJECTED", got: { v: Verdict; detail: string }) => {
    n++;
    const ok = want === "REJECTED" ? got.v !== "TICK" : got.v === want;
    if (!ok) bad++;
    console.log(`${ok ? "✓" : "✗"} ${name}: ${got.v}${want === "REJECTED" ? " (must be rejected)" : ""} — ${got.detail}`);
  };

  if (selfTest) {
    // Three probes that must be REJECTED and one that must tick, so a harness
    // that rejects everything cannot pass its own self-test.
    report("self-test: a no-op mutation must not count as caught", "REJECTED", judgeMutation({ name: "no-op", find: "const WINDOW = 40;", replace: "const WINDOW = 40; ", needle: "type annotation" }));
    report("self-test: a crash must be classified CRASH", "REJECTED", judgeMutation({ name: "crash", find: "const WINDOW = 40;", replace: "const WINDOW = ;", needle: "type annotation" }));
    report("self-test: the wrong control's failure must not count", "REJECTED", judgeMutation({ name: "wrong", find: "const WINDOW = 40;", replace: "const WINDOW = 4;", needle: "a GitHub token" }));
    report("self-test: a correct probe must tick", "TICK", judgeMutation(MUTATIONS.find((m) => m.name === "JSON-escaped home rule removed")!));
    finish(bad === 0, n, bad);
  }

  writeFileSync(MUTANT, SRC);
  const base = run(MUTANT, ["--controls-only"]);
  report("baseline: the unmutated copy passes its controls", "TICK", base.crash ? { v: "CRASH", detail: base.out.slice(-3).join(" | ") } : base.verdict === "CONTROLS-PASS" && base.exit === 0 ? { v: "TICK", detail: base.out.find((l) => l.startsWith("controls:")) ?? "" } : { v: "NOT-CAUGHT", detail: `verdict=${base.verdict}` });
  for (const m of MUTATIONS) report(`mutation: ${m.name}`, "TICK", judgeMutation(m));

  const treeBase = run(GATE, ["--tree-only"]);
  const clean = !treeBase.crash && treeBase.verdict === "PASS" && treeBase.exit === 0;
  report("baseline: the real tree passes before anything is planted", "TICK", clean ? { v: "TICK", detail: "PASS" } : { v: "NOT-CAUGHT", detail: `verdict=${treeBase.verdict} exit=${treeBase.exit}; tree plants cannot be judged against a failing baseline` });
  if (clean) for (const p of plants()) report(`plant: ${p.name}`, "TICK", judgePlant(p));
  else console.log("  (tree plants SKIPPED: a FAIL against a failing baseline proves nothing about the plant)");
  finish(bad === 0, n, bad);
}
/**
 * scrub-personal-data.ts — replace this machine's personal paths, and email
 * addresses the secret sweep does not allow, with placeholders.
 *
 *   bun run tools/scrub-personal-data.ts <file>...            # dry run: counts only
 *   bun run tools/scrub-personal-data.ts --write <file>...    # rewrite in place
 *
 * # Why this exists
 *
 * `make check-no-secrets` fails on a home directory, a macOS per-user temp
 * directory or a personal email address anywhere in what would be published.
 * Review transcripts and build logs pick those up every time a tool prints its
 * working directory, so scrubbing is a repeated task, not a one-off — and a
 * repeated hand edit is how a copy gets missed (`CLAUDE.md`: grep the phrase,
 * not the line).
 *
 * # What it replaces
 *
 * Computed at run time from the machine, so this file names no person:
 *
 * | placeholder | what |
 * |---|---|
 * | `$REPO` | this repository's root, plain or JSON-escaped |
 * | `$REPO_SLUG` | the root dash-encoded, as tools name a directory after a path |
 * | `$HOME` | the home directory, plain or JSON-escaped |
 * | `$HOME_SLUG` | the home directory dash-encoded |
 * | `$USER_TMP` | the macOS per-user temp root under `/var/folders` |
 * | `redacted@example.invalid` | every email address `check-no-secrets.ts` does not allow |
 *
 * ⚠ **Not angle brackets.** The first version wrote `<home>`, and GitHub
 * renders a bare `<home>` in Markdown as an HTML tag and drops it, so a
 * scrubbed path would have read as `/.nvm/...`. Shell-style names render
 * literally and, in a command, are still valid shell.
 *
 * Emails are found by **the gate's own `emailHits()`**, so the two cannot
 * disagree about what an address is. Afterwards the gate's path and email
 * detectors run over the result, and anything left — another user's home, a
 * shape no rule covers — is printed and the run exits 1, so it is handled by
 * hand rather than left in place.
 *
 * # What it will not do
 *
 * * **Edit code.** A path in a `.ts`, `.rs`, `.py`, `.sh` or `.js` file is a
 *   bug to fix, not text to redact: a scrubbed path no longer runs. Those files
 *   are refused unless `--allow-code` is given.
 * * **Print a value.** Counts per rule, per file, only.
 * * **Touch a secret.** Seeds, scalars and credentials are not personal data and
 *   must not be "scrubbed" into a placeholder that hides that they were there.
 *   Rotate or remove those by hand.
 */

import { readFileSync, realpathSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { homedir, tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { emailHits, pathHits } from "./check-no-secrets.ts";

const CODE = /\.(ts|tsx|js|mjs|cjs|rs|py|sh)$/;

function substitutions(): { rule: string; from: string; to: string }[] {
  const repo = realpathSync(join(import.meta.dir, ".."));
  const home = realpathSync(homedir());
  const subs: { rule: string; from: string; to: string }[] = [];
  const t = realpathSync(tmpdir()).match(/^(\/private)?(\/var\/folders\/[^/]+\/[^/]+)/);
  if (t) {
    subs.push({ rule: "user-tmp", from: "/private" + t[2], to: "$USER_TMP" });
    subs.push({ rule: "user-tmp", from: t[2], to: "$USER_TMP" });
  }
  const esc = (p: string) => p.replaceAll("/", "\\/");
  const dash = (p: string) => p.replaceAll("/", "-");
  subs.push(
    { rule: "repo", from: esc(repo), to: "$REPO" },
    { rule: "repo", from: repo, to: "$REPO" },
    { rule: "repo-slug", from: dash(repo), to: "$REPO_SLUG" },
    { rule: "home", from: esc(home), to: "$HOME" },
    { rule: "home", from: home, to: "$HOME" },
    { rule: "home-slug", from: dash(home) + "-", to: "$HOME_SLUG-" },
  );
  // Longest first, so the repository root wins over the home directory it is under.
  return subs.sort((a, b) => b.from.length - a.from.length);
}

export function scrub(text: string): { text: string; counts: Map<string, number> } {
  const counts = new Map<string, number>();
  let out = text;
  for (const s of substitutions()) {
    const n = out.split(s.from).length - 1;
    if (n) { counts.set(s.rule, (counts.get(s.rule) ?? 0) + n); out = out.replaceAll(s.from, s.to); }
  }
  const emails = emailHits(out).filter((h) => !h.allowedBy).sort((a, b) => b.at - a.at);
  for (const h of emails) out = out.slice(0, h.at) + "redacted@example.invalid" + out.slice(h.at + h.len!);
  if (emails.length) counts.set("email", emails.length);
  return { text: out, counts };
}

if (import.meta.main) {
  const argv = process.argv.slice(2);
  const write = argv.includes("--write");
  const allowCode = argv.includes("--allow-code");
  const files = argv.filter((a) => !a.startsWith("--"));
  if (!files.length) { console.log("usage: scrub-personal-data.ts [--write] [--allow-code] <file>..."); process.exit(2); }
  let left = 0, changed = 0;
  const total = new Map<string, number>();
  console.log("| file | substitutions |\n|---|---|");
  for (const f of files) {
    if (CODE.test(f) && !allowCode) { console.log(`| \`${f}\` | REFUSED: code file, fix the path by hand |`); left++; continue; }
    const before = readFileSync(f, "latin1");
    const { text, counts } = scrub(before);
    const remaining = [...pathHits(text), ...emailHits(text)].filter((h) => !h.allowedBy);
    if (counts.size) {
      changed++;
      for (const [k, n] of counts) total.set(k, (total.get(k) ?? 0) + n);
      console.log(`| \`${f}\` | ${[...counts].map(([k, n]) => `${k} ${n}`).join(", ")} |`);
      if (write) writeFileSync(f, text, "latin1");
    }
    for (const h of remaining) { left++; console.log(`| \`${f}\` | LEFT: ${h.why} — ${h.shown} |`); }
  }
  console.log(`\n${write ? "rewrote" : "would rewrite"} ${changed} file(s); totals: ${[...total].map(([k, n]) => `${k} ${n}`).join(", ") || "none"}; ${left} item(s) need a hand fix`);
  process.exit(left ? 1 : 0);
}

# --- upstream filing discipline -----------------------------------------------
# ⚠ `docs/UPSTREAM-FILING-PROTOCOL.md`, ruled 2026-09-01 after auditing the
# 2026-08-26 round: an adversarial review run AFTER submission found 13 defects,
# six graded "would embarrass", and four of five issues needed same-day public
# corrections. The 2026-09-01 audit found every claim still stands at upstream
# HEAD — so the research was sound and the ORDER OF OPERATIONS was the defect.
#
#   make check-filing FILE=docs/upstream-filings/<date>/NN-name.md
#
# Five checks: a prose word cap the gate prints, a defect stated in the opening, no
# unsearched negative claim, something that actually needs doing, and no project
# shorthand or internal register reference travelling outward.
#
# ⚠ It is LEXICAL and CANNOT tell whether a claim is true — every defect in the
# 2026-08-26 round was a false or overstated claim, and not one would have been
# caught by counting words. The protocol's rule 1 (adversarial review BEFORE
# filing) is the instrument for that; this gate only stops shapes that have
# already gone wrong twice.
#
# ⚠ `docs/upstream-filings/TEMPLATE.md` is the POSITIVE CONTROL: it must PASS.
# If it starts failing, the gate has drifted and the defect is in the gate.
# ⚠ No LIVE file may assert a retracted DISCLOSURE.
#
# ⚠⚠ Built 2026-09-02 because across FOUR review rounds the dominant defect was
# one shape: a correction reaching the instance someone was looking at, and not
# the PHRASE. "It hides AMOUNTS. It hides nothing else" survived at SIX sites
# after being fixed once — and CLAIMS.md E-136 had already corrected exactly
# that claim, at one site, weeks earlier. CLAUDE.md carries three clauses of
# advice about grepping the phrase; all three are advice, and advice is what
# kept failing (E-224).
#
# Sibling of check-zeroize-bounds part 4, for disclosures. Verified by breaking
# it three ways: reinstating a retracted sentence FAILS; emptying the phrase
# list REFUSES rather than passing; neutering the detector is caught by its own
# controls, which run through the same function the real check calls.
check-disclosure-phrases:
	@bun run tools/check-disclosure-phrases.ts

check-filing:
	@bun run tools/check-filing.ts $(FILE)

# --- phase 1, the secret sweep --------------------------------------------------
# ⚠⚠ **THE GATE ON PUBLICATION** (`docs/PLAN-PUBLIC-LAUNCH-2026-09-22.md` phase 1):
# no seed, ElGamal secret, credential, secret-store file, home path, per-user temp
# path or personal email in what `git add -A` would commit, and no secret anywhere
# in history. Personal data in history is REPORTED, not failed, because history
# is squashed at publication (E-309).
#
# ⚠ **It REFUSES without the local secret store**, because an unlabelled ElGamal
# secret is a bare 64-hex string and only an exact-value search can find one.
# `check-no-secrets-shapes` runs without it for CI and says so in its verdict
# word; that run is NOT the phase 1 gate.
#
# Its controls build a throwaway repository with planted secrets and run the same
# scanRepo() over it every time. `break-no-secrets-gate` mutates the detector
# and requires the controls to catch each mutation. For one exact tree:
#   bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts --repo <clone> --rev <commit>
check-no-secrets:
	@bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts

check-no-secrets-shapes:
	@bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts --no-local-store

	@bun run tools/break-no-secrets-gate.ts

# Secret sweep, 2026-10-18: what it found, and what was changed

Phase 1 of `docs/PLAN-PUBLIC-LAUNCH-2026-09-22.md` is gated on *"the secret sweep
passing on the exact tree that will be published"*. The sweep is
`make check-no-secrets` (`tools/check-no-secrets.ts`). This file records its first
run over the real repository, the scrub that made it pass, and what the scrub
changed. The register row is `CLAIMS.md` E-317.

## What the gate searched

The publication set (every file `git add -A` would commit), every blob, commit
message and tag message reachable from any ref, and every path any commit touched.
The known-value class searched for the exact values in the local secret store:
`state.json` in both locations `tools/rebuild-env.sh` keeps it, plus the
facility's state, book and book backup. It searched each seed in five encodings,
including the ElGamal key the ruled derivation gives it.

From the gate's own output on the final tree:

```text
controls: 51/51 passed, run through the same scanRepo() as the real check
known-value: 19 secret(s) from 5 file(s) in the local store, 67 encodings searched; 0 stored seed(s) skipped as public by construction
scanned: 613 file(s) in the tree, 2543 history object(s)
check-no-secrets: verdict=PASS exit=0
```

## What it found

- **No secret, anywhere.** None of the 19 local secrets appears in the tree or in
  history, in any encoding the gate searches. No credential shape appears either.
- **No secret-store file was ever committed.** No commit on any ref ever touched
  a path named `state.json`, `facility-state.json`, `facility-book.json`,
  `profile.json`, `.env` or a key file. With the line above, this verifies the
  plan's claim two ways rather than assuming it. Neither the path nor any value
  from `/tmp/wconf/state.json` ever entered git.
- **Every seed in the tree is public by construction.** Eleven checksum-valid
  seeds appear, and the gate decoded each one. Each has entropy from a published
  rule: one byte repeated, the bytes `0x00` to `0x0f`, or rippled's genesis
  passphrase.
- **Personal paths in 42 tracked files**: the home directory, the macOS
  per-user temp root, and the repository's dash-encoded scratchpad name. 40 of
  them are review transcripts and outputs, mostly Codex and Gemini legs that
  printed their working directory. One is the F-12 evidence README. One was code,
  a spike with the repository root hardcoded.
- **Third-party email addresses in 4 review files**, one of which had no path.
  They came from `git log` output and crate metadata that legs printed. None is
  material to a finding.

## What was changed

1. **The spike was fixed, not scrubbed**: it now computes its root
   (commit `2667870`). A redacted path in code no longer runs.
2. **42 files were scrubbed**, 41 under `docs/reviews/` and the F-12 README, by
   `tools/scrub-personal-data.ts`, which derives
   every substitution from the machine and finds emails with the gate's own
   detector. `scrub-tracked-files.md` is its output, with per-file counts and no
   values. Every changed file kept its line count. A word diff of the result
   showed that every added token carries a placeholder.

   | placeholder | replaces |
   |---|---|
   | `$REPO` | the repository root |
   | `$REPO_SLUG` | the root, dash-encoded |
   | `$HOME` | the home directory |
   | `$USER_TMP` | the macOS per-user temp root |
   | `redacted@example.invalid` | a third-party email address |

3. **The F-12 build logs were committed, scrubbed.** The handover listed 42 of
   them as home-path hits the sweep should expect. The count was right and the
   premise was not: **no log was in the publication tree**, because `*.log` is
   gitignored and only the README was ever committed. So the README beside them
   cited 57 log files that a reader of the repository could not open. All 57 are now committed under one `.gitignore` negation for that
   directory alone. 46 changed in the scrub: 42 carried the home directory and
   more carried the per-user temp root. `scrub-emsdk-logs.md` is the table.

## What the scrub costs, measured

- **Three recorded hashes no longer reproduce from the scrubbed text.**
  `logs/06-runner-scripts.log` lists five runner scripts with their sha256.
  Re-hashing each listing from the original log reproduces all five. From the
  scrubbed log, `install-emsdk.sh` and `run-group.sh` still reproduce, and
  `run-build.sh`, `run-test.sh` and `run-xtrace.sh` do not. Each of those three
  had the home directory on its `NODE22=` line. The F-12 README says so where it
  prints the scripts.
- **No other recorded hash is affected.** The undefined-symbol set hashes in
  `logs/08-gen-tables-and-symbol-sets.log` are over symbol names; that log's
  only two changed lines are the paths in a `shasum` listing. The build-output
  hashes are over binary outputs, and only the paths beside them changed.
- **The originals are kept, locally, unpublished**, at
  `docs/evidence/f12-emsdk-2026-10-18/logs-unscrubbed/`, which stays ignored. If
  a negation or a `git add -f` ever brought them into the tree, the gate would fail
  on them. `emsdk-logs-unscrubbed.sha256` and `emsdk-logs-scrubbed.sha256` record
  both sides.

## A decision this took, and why it is reversible

`docs/reviews/` is a historical record, and the launch plan publishes it. 41
review files had personal paths or emails redacted. **That changes the bytes of a
record and not what any leg was shown to mean**: a path under the home directory is
still a path under the home directory. The private archive keeps every original in
history, which is not rewritten. The squash ruling (`CLAIMS.md` E-309) means history
is not published. Vocabulary in `docs/reviews/` is untouched. Whether the
`tapFailHard` translation reaches those files is still the owner's decision.

## What this does not establish

- **Personal data is still in history.** The gate reports 1,045 path hits and 30
  email hits in old blobs, and does not fail on them, because history is squashed
  at publication. If anyone proposes publishing the history,
  `bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts --strict-history` fails on them, and history
  would need rewriting first.
- **The gate is lexical, over named classes.** `check-no-secrets` prints what it
  cannot see on every run. That covers a secret split across literals, any
  re-encoding, an unlabelled scalar that is not in the local store, and hostnames
  and names. Each limit is an asserted-uncaught control, so the list cannot go
  stale silently.
- **The publication commit has not been scanned, because it does not exist yet.**
  Phase 1 item 5 creates it, and the gate's `--repo <clone> --rev <commit>` form
  runs on exactly that tree. That run is the phase 1 gate, and this one is not.
- **The gate is not externally reviewed yet.** The launch plan asks for one
  review round before the gate is trusted for publication. It is
  `REVIEW-056`.

| R-1 | TASK ZERO §9 rev 1: the restructured facility "shows nothing about which supplier drew how much" | Amounts are hidden; counterparty graph, event timing and drawdown frequency are public | Found independently by all three review passes. Sender and receiver are public on every transfer |
| R-2 | ADR-0001 rev 1: local proving is "the only option under which the confidentiality claim is true" | Local proving preserves spending authority; issuer visibility is unchanged | Conflated custody with confidentiality — the exact distinction the ADR exists to draw |
| R-3 | F-3: "docs say confidentiality can be toggled on and off" | Withdrawn 2026-08-16 | Corrected upstream. The canonical xrpl.org page states enabling is one-way |
| R-4 | F-4: "docs say clawback resets the version counter" | Withdrawn 2026-08-16 | Not a doc error. TOB-RIPCTX-11, a Medium implementation bug found by Trail of Bits in April 2026 and since Resolved. The prerelease docs described pre-fix behaviour |
| R-6 | B-11 / ADR-0002 blocker 3 / investigation §12: that encrypted loan accounting is **structurally** blocked, because XLS-96's additive homomorphism "cannot support amortisation, which multiplies a balance by a rate" | Withdrawn 2026-08-17. The residual true statement is B-11 as rewritten | **The mathematics was wrong.** Scalar multiplication by a *public* scalar is native to an additively homomorphic scheme: for `(C₁,C₂) = (rG, mG+rP)`, scaling both components by public `k` yields `(krG, kmG+krP)`, which decrypts to `k·m`. An interest rate is a public loan parameter. What is genuinely non-linear is rounding and fixed-point truncation — and the remedy for that is a per-payment ZK proof, which is the pattern XLS-96 already uses. **Found independently by both REVIEW-003 legs**, and verified algebraically before acceptance. It was graded C and its inference was stated, which is what made it checkable — but a stated inference that is wrong is still wrong |
| R-5 | TASK ZERO §9: the vault converts the whole facility float in one transaction | ~~Retracted 2026-08-16 per ADDENDUM §1~~ — **un-retracted 2026-08-16 (same day), see F-7** | The retraction was correct about the mechanism and wrong about the consequence. A 10,000,000-unit balance is **not** undecryptable: decryption takes a caller-supplied window, and the vault — which tracks its own float off-ledger anyway — decrypts it in ~5 ms with a narrow window. §9's single-conversion design **stands**. What must be added is not a float ceiling but a **balance-tracking requirement** and a resynchronisation cost budget |

## Claim under test

`.github/scripts/build-wasm.sh` in XRPLF/mpt-crypto compiles `src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp` with `em++` (line 272) but links with plain `emcc` on lines 363, 365 and 374 at commit `3baaaac`. Emscripten 6.0.6 disabled `DEFAULT_TO_CXX` by default. So the link fails from 6.0.6 on, and switching the three link lines to `em++` or adding `-sDEFAULT_TO_CXX` fixes it.

## Differences from the claim

1. At the newest emsdk, 6.0.12, the unpatched build still fails at line 363. But it reports 10 undefined symbols, not 8. The two extra symbols are `__cxa_guard_acquire` and `__cxa_guard_release`, and they print before the claim's 8. At 6.0.6 and 6.0.10 the set is exactly the claim's 8. The sorted symbol list hashes the same in all three 8-symbol logs: 6.0.6 unpatched, 6.0.10 unpatched and 6.0.6 partial (`logs/08-gen-tables-and-symbol-sets.log`). Why 6.0.12 adds two was not investigated. 6.0.11 was not run.
2. At 6.0.10 and 6.0.12 every link also prints `WASM_BIGINT is deprecated`, because the script passes `-sWASM_BIGINT=1`. It appears once on a failed build and three times on a successful one. It does not fail the build. The deprecation is listed under 6.0.8 in the 6.0.12 ChangeLog (line 97).
3. The ChangeLog shipped inside the 6.0.6 install heads that section `6.0.6 (in development)` (line 21). The entry is at lines 23-25. The dated header `6.0.6 - 08/05/26` only appears in later installs, at line 120 in the 6.0.12 file. A citation should name which file it quotes.
4. Every other part of the claim reproduced: the 6.0.2 control, the builds at 6.0.3 to 6.0.5, the 6.0.6 and 6.0.10 failures at line 363 (warning text included), both fixes at 6.0.2, 6.0.6, 6.0.10 and 6.0.12, byte identity at 6.0.2, and the partial patch failing at line 374.
5. Platform: this ran on macOS arm64 with bash 3.2, not on CI's `ubuntu-latest`. See the environment section.

How this differs from CI (`.github/workflows/build-wasm.yml` at `3baaaac`):

- CI runs on `ubuntu-latest`. This run used macOS arm64.
- CI installs emsdk with `mymindstorm/setup-emsdk@v14` (`version: 6.0.2`). This run cloned emsdk (main at `1c9a998ee5dd2e38aec0c5abbf23dffdb8f10d57`, 2026-10-16) once per version and ran `./emsdk install <v>` and `./emsdk activate <v>`.
- CI's `actions/setup-node` gives the newest Node 22. This run used 22.22.3.
- CI uses the runner's bash 5. This run used bash 3.2.
- The CI steps "Package WASM bundle" and "Upload bundle" were not run.

docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:14:- **Scope:** ADR-0001 §(e) keygen half — deterministic derivation of the holder's ElGamal keypair ($sk_A, pk_A$) from the XRPL root master seed.  
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:22:In REVIEW-010c, derivation of the ElGamal encryption randomness $r$ from the long-lived secret $sk_A$ was rejected on the definitive ground that:
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:25:On `ConfidentialMPTConvert` and `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack`, the value $r$ is published on-chain in cleartext in the `BlindingFactor` field (`CLAIMS.md` E-67). Deriving a publicly disclosed value from a long-lived secret unnecessarily forces reliance on the pseudorandom function (PRF) assumption under public oracle exposure, while providing **zero** recovery benefit (since historical $r_b$ is not needed to prove subsequent transfers; balance linkage is established using $sk_A$ via $B_2 - b \cdot G = sk_A \cdot B_1$, `CLAIMS.md` E-33).
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:27:**For the ElGamal private key $sk_A$, the logic of REVIEW-010c does not merely fail to transfer — it completely inverts:**
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:29:1. **No Cleartext Disclosure:** $sk_A$ is never published in cleartext or in any oracle-evaluatable format on-chain. The only public value is $pk_A = sk_A \cdot G$, which is the standard discrete-logarithm hard relation underpinning all elliptic-curve public-key cryptography.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:31:3. **Decisive Asymmetry of Failure Modes:** For $r$, fresh entropy is free and deterministic derivation buys nothing. For $sk_A$, deterministic derivation buys the **sole operational disaster-recovery mechanism** available to a holder in a protocol where key rotation is impossible (`tecDUPLICATE`, `CLAIMS.md` E-9) and key loss results in permanent, unrecoverable token lockup (spec §A.6, `CLAIMS.md` E-10, P-10).
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:41:- **The Recovery Trade-off:** In XLS-0096, an independently generated, non-derived $sk_A$ requires an independent backup separate from the holder's XRPL master seed. If this secondary backup is lost or corrupted, the holder's confidential balance is permanently destroyed (the only protocol remedy being issuer clawback/burn, `CLAIMS.md` E-12). In contrast, deriving $sk_A$ from the master seed guarantees that a standard XRPL account backup restores both spending authority and confidential balance access.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:77:                    │   sk_A = (X mod (q - 1)) + 1           │
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:78:                    │   assert sk_A != 0                     │

docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:146:   $sk_A$ can never equal $0$, and is strictly less than the group order $q$.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:153:4. **Zero Check & Failure Handling:** If, due to hardware fault or library corruption, the computed $sk_A = 0$, the signer MUST immediately **ABORT** and refuse to output key material.

docs/reviews/REVIEW-012a-gemini-keygen-2026-08-18.md:185:- **Regular Keys (`SetRegularKey`):** A regular key is an authorized delegate that can sign transactions, but does not own the account and can be rotated or deleted at any time. Because `HolderEncryptionKey` registration is permanent (`tecDUPLICATE`), $sk_A$ **MUST NEVER** be derived from a Regular Key seed. It MUST always be derived from the account's root master seed.

docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-REQUEST-keygen-2026-08-18.md:49:REVIEW-010c's ground for rejecting `sk_A`-derived `r`:
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-REQUEST-keygen-2026-08-18.md:55:**An ElGamal private key is never published.** Nothing about it is disclosed on-chain except `pk_A = sk_A·G`, which is the public key by construction. The disclosure argument that decided `r` **has no counterpart here**. The analogy may therefore run the other way, and this packet is not asking you to be consistent with the last ruling — it is asking you to decide this question on its own terms.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-REQUEST-keygen-2026-08-18.md:163:**`CLAIMS.md` is attached in full**, and is the register of what this project believes and on what evidence. A packet two rounds ago quoted seven of its identifiers as authority while telling the reviewer not to read it, so the reviewer saw conclusions without evidence. **Read it or ignore it — do not take a grade on trust.** Rows most likely to matter: **P-5, P-9, P-10** (standing prohibitions), **E-9, E-10** (no rotation, no recovery), **E-33** (`sk_A` is a proof witness), **E-34, F-17** (nonce reuse extracts `sk_A`), **E-47, E-53** (keygen not exported), **E-63** (the executed `r`-reuse attack), **E-66, E-67, F-19** (new today).
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:15:**Why the `r`/`ρ` ruling does not govern here.** That ruling forbade deriving `r` from a long-lived secret because `r` is **published in clear** in `BlindingFactor`, so derivation would publish PRF outputs keyed by holder secret material and buy nothing in return. Neither half of that reasoning holds for `sk_A`: it is never published, and derivation buys the **only** recovery mechanism available in a protocol with no rotation and no recovery. **The conclusion inverts.**
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:17:**But state the reason precisely, because a nearby version of it is false.** It is *not* true that nothing seed-derived is published. Under this construction `pk_A = sk_A·G` is published permanently and welded to the account by the registration PoK (E-66). The distinction that carries the ruling is narrower: **one published group element at one fixed label is not an oracle.** An oracle is queryable — the `r` case gives an adversary a growing family of PRF outputs at inputs they influence by choosing when to transact. Here there is exactly one output, per account, forever, and recovering the seed from it requires solving a discrete log and then inverting HKDF.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:64:sk_A  = (X mod (n − 1)) + 1                                        // in [1, n−1], TOTAL
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:65:pk_A  = compressed_SEC1(sk_A · G)                                  // 33 bytes
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:72:`sk_A = (X mod (n−1)) + 1` **cannot produce zero and needs no branch.** There is no abort path, no retry, and no secret-dependent control flow.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:112:- Plaintext seed and `sk_A` are **never** written to non-volatile storage.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:114:- Seed, `PRK`, `OKM64` and `sk_A` buffers are pinned against swap where the OS allows, and zeroized on drop and on `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`/panic.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:116:- **The claim stops here.** None of it defeats an adversary with code execution, who reads `sk_A` from process memory. P-5 forbids calling it secure.

docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:123:2. **No endpoint returns `sk_A` or any derived secret.**

docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:182:Under this construction that retired seed **still derives `sk_A`**, and therefore still yields full retroactive decryption of the entire confidential history, permanently, with no rotation. Three consequences:
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:198:**4. `1 ≤ sk_A ≤ n−1` guaranteed without a secret-dependent branch** (§2.3).
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:200:**5. A derivation KAT, distinct from upstream's interop vectors.** `test_pok_sk`, `test_elgamal` and `test_elgamal_verify` establish that a keypair is **well-formed and interoperates** — a constant `sk_A = 1` passes all three. They establish **nothing** about whether the key was derived correctly. `CLAUDE.md`'s requirement to validate against them stands and is necessary; it is not sufficient, and the derivation needs its own vectors, reproduced by an implementation written from this prose rather than from reference control flow.
docs/reviews/REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md:206:1. **Master seed compromise.** The attacker derives `sk_A` and decrypts the holder's entire confidential history, retroactively and permanently. This is the accepted cost of the ruling, not an oversight.

In a document whose entire premise is byte-exactness, a misattributed audit finding is not cosmetic. It should be corrected in `CLAIMS.md` wherever E-48/E-55 depend on it.

### 5.5 Also worth fixing

- **C-4 will be misread.** *"The signer must never accept caller-supplied randomness across its localhost interface"* is correct and should stay. But a literal implementer will bury the `getrandom` call inside the derivation function to comply, which destroys known-answer testability and makes T-5(a) impossible to write. Add the clarifying half-sentence: the prohibition is on the **HTTP boundary**, not the internal function signature.
- **ConvertBack witness order.** C-1 says `(b, ρ, sk_A)`; the paper's §8.3 relation says `(b, sk_A, ρ)` and its nonce sampling says `(t_b, t_sk, t_ρ)`, while its serialisation says `(e, z_b, z_ρ, z_sk)`. C-1 agrees with the serialisation. Harmless under C-6, but the packet presents it as sourced from the paper when the paper says three things.
- **The §4 / §6 numbering mismatch** between the cover instruction ("Section 6… ten items") and the packet (§4, twelve items). Noted in §2.
- **§5 is still, mildly, steering** — one paragraph for hedging, one longer against, and item 12 warns only about the hedged failure mode with no equivalent warning that a deterministic construction whose preimage is coarser than the challenge preimage fails *catastrophically and unconditionally*. §9 records that the two legs disagreed about direction. On a cold read the residual lean is toward deterministic. It did not change the answer, but it is there.
- **The framing invites elaboration rather than checking.** "Not implementable as written" and "produce the construction that replaces it" both point at completeness. The sketch's defect is not completeness — it is that `statement` is the wrong input set (§3.1). A reviewer who dutifully filled in twelve items around `HKDF(sk_A ‖ r ‖ m ‖ b ‖ ρ ‖ statement)` would ship the bug with every box ticked. Worth recording as a lesson about how review packets should state the ask: *"specify this completely"* and *"check whether this is right"* are different requests, and the first one crowds out the second.

## 6. Information I was not given

Named rather than guessed, per the instruction.

| ID | Gap | Why it blocks | How to close |
|---|---|---|---|
| **GAP-A** | **Does the signer generate nonces, or does `mpt-crypto` over FFI?** | Determines whether §1 is implementable code or dead code (§5.1). | A one-line answer from the build plan. Then either implement §1 (Branch B) or convert this ruling into a verification-and-guard task (Branch A). |
| **GAP-B** | **The Fiat–Shamir preimage of the registration PoK (`CMPT_POK_SK_REGISTER`).** Not in the paper — §8 covers Convert, Clawback, ConvertBack and nothing else. | The `FS_PREFIX` row in §1.6 is provisional. I will not guess it. | Read the registration verifier in rippled and the corresponding prover in `mpt-crypto`. Specifically needed: the ordered element list; whether the `AccountID` and `MPTokenIssuanceID` bind in; and whether a `TransactionContextID` is present at all. **If the account does not bind in, raise it separately** — a PoK that does not bind the registering account is copyable to another account, which is a weaker rogue-key defence than ADR §(b) claims. That is a question for the keygen ruling, not this one. |
| **GAP-C** | **Which proof format does devnet rippled 3.3.0 accept — commitment or compact?** | §5.3. Determines whether §1.6's rows are the right rows. | Check the proof-size constants in the shipped serialisation layer and the amendment gating the compact format, with a dated `feature` RPC call. Do not infer it from `secp256k1_mpt.h`; `mpt-crypto` retains both forms, and per §8.5 the compact variants are additive. |
| **GAP-D** | **What upstream's 32-byte `statement_hash` covers.** | If it excludes `TransactionContextID`, §3.1's bug exists upstream and is masked only by the salt. Directly determines a `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK` entry's severity. | Read the call sites of `generate_deterministic_nonces` at the pinned commit. |
| **GAP-E** | **Where `r`, `ρ`, and the Bulletproof randomness come from.** | §5.2. | Same read; then decide whether they fall under this construction or under upstream's. |

## Appendix A — test vectors

Generated by the reference implementation shipped alongside this ruling. All values are 32-byte scalars in `[1, q)`, hex, big-endian.

**Common material** (deterministic, and deliberately unrealistic so it cannot be mistaken for real key material):

```
sk_A = 0x…07     sk_iss = 0x…11
m    = 1234567
r    = 0x02b1f8c0d9e4a5713c8f60b2d3a94e7561fb08c2d7e93a4150c6b8d2f3e5a7091
b    = 98765432100
rho  = 0x0c9d3a7e15b284f6039e7c1d5a8b3f240761c9e83d5a2b4f108e6c3d97a5b201

P_A  = 025cbdf0646e5db4eaa398f365f2ea7a0e3d419b7e0330e39ce92bddedcac4f9bc

Require decoded.bytes length = 16.
Require decoded.type is exactly "secp256k1" or "ed25519".
Derive root AccountID from ripple-keypairs.deriveKeypair(family_seed).
If an expected AccountID is supplied, require equality.

IKM = LABEL
   || u32be(len(decoded.type)) || decoded.type
   || u32be(16)                || decoded.bytes
   || u32be(20)                || root_account_id

PRK    = HMAC-SHA512(key = SALT, msg = IKM)
INFO   = EXPAND || u32be(len(ROLE)) || ROLE
OKM64  = HMAC-SHA512(key = PRK, msg = INFO || 0x01)
sk_A   = int_be(OKM64) mod N
```

If `sk_A == 0`, abort onboarding and do not retry with altered labels or counters. Otherwise encode `sk_A` as 32-byte big-endian. `pk_A` is compressed secp256k1 `sk_A * G`.

The registration proof must always use a non-NULL transaction context. rippled’s path binds registration to account, issuance and sequence (`docs/task-zero/SOURCES-2026-08-18.md:35`, `docs/task-zero/SOURCES-2026-08-18.md:65`), but the lower library API accepts NULL and then becomes account-unbound (`docs/task-zero/SOURCES-2026-08-18.md:67`, `docs/task-zero/SOURCES-2026-08-18.md:75`; `CLAIMS.md:161`).

## Failing Test

The future committed test must reproduce both vectors, assert that the two seed types separate, reject malformed seed length/type, reject AccountID mismatch, reject scalar zero by stubbing `OKM64 = 0^64`, and fail on any label, salt, role, framing, or reduction change.

Minimum assertion:

```ts
assert.equal(vector1.sk_A, "F523DA4F10344409A57E27EC6C2FF66E612962C4002AE347D53226FBCA26925A")
assert.equal(vector1.pk_A, "02658DF33DCE8368C1FA17D7DE828D234D9CE5593285A69BDE7338EF1A2C023EEC")
assert.equal(vector2.sk_A, "32AFE79F8CC2D1745DEE0AB5FBF2B6AF39E1F63DC5B8D83C7200A05C8F56EE51")
assert.equal(vector2.pk_A, "033CE826BEA6E591B1760885CFAA8AC6D91625A127CE9A4135F46B5BB96581D2FF")
assert.notEqual(vector1.sk_A, vector2.sk_A)
```

## Explicit Scope

This construction does not protect against compromise of the XRPL family seed, passphrase, host OS, signer binary, or signer memory. The architecture already says the local boundary raises the bar from leaked token to code execution, not to security against code execution (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:58`, `docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:66`). It does not provide holder privacy from the issuer; issuer visibility is mandatory (`CLAIMS.md:27`, `docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:43`, `docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:50`). It does not solve key rotation, recovery, or post-compromise repair (`CLAIMS.md:28`, `CLAIMS.md:46`, `CLAIMS.md:47`). It does not protect `r`, `rho`, sigma nonces, or Bulletproof blinding; those live in separate rulings or remain open (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:72`, `docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:77`).
## 5. What the signer must refuse

1. **No endpoint accepts a seed, a passphrase, `sk_A`, `r`, `ρ`, or entropy of any kind.** Carried forward from REVIEW-010c and extended to key material.
2. **No endpoint returns `sk_A` or any derived secret.**
3. **Seed entry happens only in the signer's own local UI**, never through the browser client and never over IPC.
4. **The registration PoK must always be given a non-NULL `context_id`.** The library accepts NULL and then produces an account-unbound, freely replayable registration proof (F-19). rippled's own path cannot reach that, but the signer links the library directly and can. Assert non-NULL at the call site rather than relying on the wrapper.
5. A test-only offline API may inject fixed seeds **only** if it cannot sign, submit, or emit a production transaction blob.

---

## 6. Known-answer vectors

**Computed, not asserted.** `PRK` and `OKM64` are leg B's values, which were independently recomputed during adjudication and reproduced **exactly**; only the reduction differs from leg B's, so only `sk_A` and `pk_A` changed. Both vectors are reproduced by two implementations of this prose — one calling `HMAC-SHA-512` directly, one via `@noble/hashes`' `hkdf()` — which agree.

Both vectors use seed entropy `000102030405060708090A0B0C0D0E0F`.

**The two vectors share seed entropy and differ in everything downstream**, which is the point: the same 16 bytes encode to two different family seeds, two different accounts, and therefore two different ElGamal keys.

/** What `git add -A` would commit, read from disk. Symlinks are not followed. */
export function treeUnits(repo: string): Unit[] {
  const names = gitText(repo, ["ls-files", "-z", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard"]).split("\0").filter(Boolean);
  const units: Unit[] = [];
  for (const n of [...new Set(names)]) {
    const p = join(repo, n);
    units.push({ where: n, text: n, scope: "tree", kind: "path" });
    if (!existsSync(p) && !lstatSync(p, { throwIfNoEntry: false })) {
      // Deleted on disk, still in the index: the index copy is what a plain commit publishes.
      try { units.push({ where: n, text: asText(git(repo, ["cat-file", "blob", `:${n}`])), scope: "tree", kind: "file" }); } catch { /* not in index either */ }
      continue;
    }
    const s = lstatSync(p);
    if (s.isSymbolicLink()) units.push({ where: `${n} (symlink target)`, text: readlinkSync(p), scope: "tree", kind: "file" });
    else if (s.isFile()) units.push({ where: n, text: asText(readFileSync(p)), scope: "tree", kind: "file" });
  }
  return units;
}

/** Exactly the blobs of one commit's tree. */
export function revUnits(repo: string, rev: string): Unit[] {
  const entries = gitText(repo, ["ls-tree", "-r", "-z", "--full-tree", rev]).split("\0").filter(Boolean).map((l) => {
    const [meta, path] = l.split("\t");
    return { sha: meta.split(" ")[2], path };
  });
  const byPath = new Map(entries.map((e) => [e.sha, e.path] as const));
  const units: Unit[] = entries.map((e) => ({ where: e.path, text: e.path, scope: "tree" as const, kind: "path" as const }));
  for (const o of batchObjects(repo, [...new Set(entries.map((e) => e.sha))])) units.push({ where: byPath.get(o.sha)!, text: asText(o.body), scope: "tree", kind: "file" });
  return units;
}

  const split = t.indexOf("\n\n");

codex
The baseline is clean, but two untested attack surfaces are now clear enough to exercise: Git object replacement can make Git return clean bytes for a secret-bearing committed object, and the label scanner is line- and width-sensitive. I’m adding a temporary, untracked probe driver, running it against synthetic repositories only, and then removing it; it will not touch the real store or commits.

+function run(cmd: string[], cwd = ROOT, input?: string, env = gitEnv) {
+  const r = Bun.spawnSync(cmd, { cwd, stdin: input, stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe", env: { ...process.env, ...env } });
+  return {
+    exit: r.exitCode ?? -1,
+    out: new TextDecoder().decode(r.stdout),
+    err: new TextDecoder().decode(r.stderr),
+  };
+}
+
+function g(repo: string, ...args: string[]) {
+  const r = run(["git", ...args], repo);
+  if (r.exit !== 0) throw new Error(`git ${args.join(" ")} failed: ${r.err}`);
+  return r.out.trim();
+}
+
+function init(repo: string) {
+  mkdirSync(repo, { recursive: true });
+  g(repo, "init", "-q", "-b", "main");

+function put(repo: string, rel: string, body: string) {
+  const p = join(repo, rel);
+  mkdirSync(dirname(p), { recursive: true });
+  writeFileSync(p, body);

+  const harness = run(["bun", "run", join(clone, "tools", "break-no-secrets-gate.ts")], clone);
+  put(clone, "synthetic-openai-token.txt", `sk-${"A".repeat(40)}\n`);
+  const leakRun = run(["bun", "run", mutantGate, "--tree-only", "--no-local-store"], clone);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector controls: ${controls.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector harness: ${harness.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector planted token: ${leakRun.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+} finally {
+  rmSync(scratch, { recursive: true, force: true });
+}

+import { randomBytes } from "node:crypto";
+import {
+  chmodSync, cpSync, existsSync, homedir as _unused, mkdirSync, mkdtempSync,
+  readFileSync, rmSync, symlinkSync, writeFileSync,
+} from "node:fs";
+import { homedir, tmpdir } from "node:os";
+import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
+import { encodeSeed } from "ripple-address-codec";
+import {
+  CITED_VECTORS, loadStore, pathHits, revUnits, scanRepo, scanText,
+} from "./check-no-secrets.ts";
+import { scrub } from "./scrub-personal-data.ts";
+
+const ROOT = join(import.meta.dir, "..");
+const GATE = join(import.meta.dir, "check-no-secrets.ts");
+const scratch = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "review056-"));
+const gitEnv = {
+  GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM: "1",
+  GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL: "/dev/null",
+  GIT_AUTHOR_NAME: "probe",
+  GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL: "probe@example.invalid",
+  GIT_COMMITTER_NAME: "probe",
+  GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL: "probe@example.invalid",
+};
+
+function run(cmd: string[], cwd = ROOT, input?: string, env = gitEnv) {
+  const r = Bun.spawnSync(cmd, { cwd, stdin: input, stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe", env: { ...process.env, ...env } });
+  return {
+    exit: r.exitCode ?? -1,
+    out: new TextDecoder().decode(r.stdout),
+    err: new TextDecoder().decode(r.stderr),
+  };
+}
+
+function g(repo: string, ...args: string[]) {
+  const r = run(["git", ...args], repo);
+  if (r.exit !== 0) throw new Error(`git ${args.join(" ")} failed: ${r.err}`);
+  return r.out.trim();
+}
+
+function init(repo: string) {
+  mkdirSync(repo, { recursive: true });
+  g(repo, "init", "-q", "-b", "main");
+}
+
+function put(repo: string, rel: string, body: string) {
+  const p = join(repo, rel);
+  mkdirSync(dirname(p), { recursive: true });
+  writeFileSync(p, body);
+}
+
+function gate(args: string[]) {
+  const r = run(["bun", "run", GATE, ...args], ROOT);
+  const lines = r.out.trimEnd().split("\n");
+  return { exit: r.exit, last: lines.at(-1) ?? "", scanned: lines.find((l) => l.startsWith("scanned:")) ?? "" };
+}
+
+const emptyStore = { files: [], needles: [], secrets: 0, publicSkipped: 0 };
+const scalar64 = "a".repeat(64);
+const scalar63 = "a".repeat(63);
+
+try {
+  console.log("DETECTOR SHAPES");
+  const shapes: [string, string][] = [
+    ["63-digit labelled scalar", `private_key: ${scalar63}`],
+    ["pretty JSON scalar on next line", `{"private_key":\n  "${scalar64}"}`],
+    ["Markdown value before label", `| ${scalar64} | private key |`],
+    ["pretty JSON seed entropy on next line", `{"seed":\n  "${randomBytes(16).toString("hex")}"}`],
+    ["Windows forward-slash home", "C:[synthetic personal-path redacted]/work"],
+    ["AWS secret access key", `aws_secret_access_key=${"A".repeat(40)}`],
+  ];
+  for (const [name, text] of shapes) console.log(`${name}: findings=${scanText(text, emptyStore).filter((h) => !h.allowedBy).length}`);
+
+  const toy = `private_key: ${"0".repeat(63)}7`;
+  const publicSeed = encodeSeed(Buffer.alloc(16, 0x42), "ed25519");
+  const toyHits = scanText(toy, emptyStore);
+  const publicHits = scanText(`production_seed=${publicSeed}`, emptyStore);
+  console.log(`toy scalar: findings=${toyHits.filter((h) => !h.allowedBy).length} allowed=${toyHits.some((h) => h.allowedBy?.includes("toy scalar"))}`);
+  console.log(`repeated-byte production seed: findings=${publicHits.filter((h) => !h.allowedBy).length} allowed=${publicHits.some((h) => h.allowedBy?.includes("public entropy"))}`);
+
+  const storeDir = join(scratch, "malformed-store");
+  mkdirSync(storeDir);
+  const storedSeed = encodeSeed(randomBytes(16), "ed25519");
+  const omittedScalar = randomBytes(32).toString("hex");
+  writeFileSync(join(storeDir, "good.json"), JSON.stringify({ seed: storedSeed }));
+  writeFileSync(join(storeDir, "broken.json"), `{"sk":"${omittedScalar}"`);
+  const malformedStore = loadStore([storeDir], {});
+  console.log(`malformed store: secrets=${malformedStore.secrets} files=${malformedStore.files.length} bare-scalar-findings=${scanText(omittedScalar, malformedStore).filter((h) => !h.allowedBy).length}`);
+
+  console.log("\nENUMERATION");
+  const filterRepo = join(scratch, "filter-repo");
+  init(filterRepo);
+  const filteredSeed = encodeSeed(randomBytes(16), "ed25519");
+  put(filterRepo, ".gitattributes", "payload.txt filter=inject\n");
+  put(filterRepo, "payload.txt", "SAFE\n");
+  put(filterRepo, "cited.txt", CITED_VECTORS.map((v) => `private_key: ${v.value}`).join("\n") + "\n");
+  g(filterRepo, "config", "filter.inject.clean", `sed s/SAFE/${filteredSeed}/g`);
+  const beforeAdd = gate(["--repo", filterRepo, "--tree-only", "--no-local-store"]);
+  g(filterRepo, "add", "-A");
+  g(filterRepo, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", "filtered");
+  const committedFiltered = g(filterRepo, "show", "HEAD:payload.txt").includes(filteredSeed);
+  const afterAdd = gate(["--repo", filterRepo, "--rev", "HEAD", "--no-local-store"]);
+  console.log(`clean filter before git add: ${beforeAdd.last}`);
+  console.log(`clean filter committed secret=${committedFiltered}; after commit: ${afterAdd.last}`);
+
+  const replaceRepo = join(scratch, "replace-repo");
+  init(replaceRepo);
+  const replacedSeed = encodeSeed(randomBytes(16), "secp256k1");
+  put(replaceRepo, "leak.txt", replacedSeed + "\n");
+  g(replaceRepo, "add", "-A");
+  g(replaceRepo, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", "secret object");
+  const oldBlob = g(replaceRepo, "rev-parse", "HEAD:leak.txt");
+  const cleanBlob = run(["git", "hash-object", "-w", "--stdin"], replaceRepo, "SAFE\n").out.trim();
+  g(replaceRepo, "replace", oldBlob, cleanBlob);
+  writeFileSync(join(replaceRepo, "leak.txt"), "SAFE\n");
+  const original = run(["git", "cat-file", "blob", oldBlob], replaceRepo, undefined, { ...gitEnv, GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS: "1" }).out.includes(replacedSeed);
+  const revReplace = gate(["--repo", replaceRepo, "--rev", "HEAD", "--no-local-store"]);
+  const histReplace = gate(["--repo", replaceRepo, "--history-only", "--no-local-store"]);
+  console.log(`replace ref original secret=${original}; rev: ${revReplace.last}; history: ${histReplace.last}`);
+
+  const tabRepo = join(scratch, "tab-repo");
+  init(tabRepo);
+  put(tabRepo, "safe\t/Users/Alice/state.json", "SAFE\n");
+  g(tabRepo, "add", "-A");
+  g(tabRepo, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", "odd path");
+  const rawPathHasSecretShape = g(tabRepo, "ls-tree", "-r", "--name-only", "HEAD").includes("state.json");
+  const tabRev = gate(["--repo", tabRepo, "--rev", "HEAD", "--no-local-store"]);
+  console.log(`tab filename contains secret path=${rawPathHasSecretShape}; rev: ${tabRev.last}`);
+
+  const sub = join(scratch, "sub");
+  init(sub);
+  put(sub, "ok.txt", "SAFE\n");
+  g(sub, "add", "-A");
+  g(sub, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", "sub");
+  const superRepo = join(scratch, "super");
+  init(superRepo);
+  g(superRepo, "-c", "protocol.file.allow=always", "submodule", "add", "-q", sub, "vendor/sub");
+  g(superRepo, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-am", "submodule");
+  const subRev = gate(["--repo", superRepo, "--rev", "HEAD", "--no-local-store"]);
+  console.log(`ordinary submodule rev: ${subRev.last}`);
+
+  const histRepo = join(scratch, "history-repo");
+  init(histRepo);
+  put(histRepo, "state.json", "{}\n");
+  g(histRepo, "add", "-A");
+  g(histRepo, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", "old path");
+  g(histRepo, "mv", "state.json", "safe.txt");
+  g(histRepo, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", "rename");
+  const tagSeed = encodeSeed(randomBytes(16), "ed25519");
+  g(histRepo, "tag", "-a", "probe-tag", "-m", `tag carries ${tagSeed}`);
+  g(histRepo, "checkout", "-q", "-b", "side", "HEAD~1");
+  put(histRepo, "side.txt", "SAFE\n");
+  g(histRepo, "add", "-A");
+  g(histRepo, "commit", "-q", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", "side");
+  g(histRepo, "checkout", "-q", "main");
+  const mergeSeed = encodeSeed(randomBytes(16), "secp256k1");
+  g(histRepo, "merge", "--no-ff", "--no-gpg-sign", "-m", `merge carries ${mergeSeed}`, "side");
+  const mergeSha = g(histRepo, "rev-parse", "HEAD").slice(0, 10);
+  const hr = scanRepo(histRepo, emptyStore, { tree: false, history: true });
+  console.log(`history rename-old-path=${hr.findings.some((f) => f.cls === "secret-file" && f.where.startsWith("state.json"))} annotated-tag=${hr.findings.some((f) => f.cls === "seed" && f.where.startsWith("tag "))} merge-message=${hr.findings.some((f) => f.cls === "seed" && f.where.startsWith(`commit ${mergeSha}`))}`);
+
+  console.log("\nSCRUBBER");
+  const unreported = `X${homedir()}/file`;
+  const scrubbed = scrub(unreported);
+  console.log(`non-detector substring: pre-path-hits=${pathHits(unreported).filter((h) => !h.allowedBy).length} changed=${scrubbed.text !== unreported}`);
+  const winFile = join(scratch, "windows.txt");
+  writeFileSync(winFile, "C:[synthetic personal-path redacted]/work\n");
+  const scrubRun = run(["bun", "run", join(import.meta.dir, "scrub-personal-data.ts"), winFile], ROOT);
+  console.log(`forward-slash Windows path scrubber exit=${scrubRun.exit}; ${scrubRun.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+
+  console.log("\nUNTESTED MUTATION");
+  const clone = join(scratch, "mutant-repo");
+  const cloneRun = run(["git", "clone", "-q", "--no-hardlinks", ROOT, clone], ROOT);
+  if (cloneRun.exit !== 0) throw new Error(cloneRun.err);
+  symlinkSync(join(ROOT, "node_modules"), join(clone, "node_modules"), "dir");
+  const mutantGate = join(clone, "tools", "check-no-secrets.ts");
+  const source = readFileSync(mutantGate, "utf8");
+  const openAiLine = '  { name: "OpenAI API key", re: /\\bsk-(?:proj-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{40,}/g },\n';
+  if (!source.includes(openAiLine)) throw new Error("OpenAI mutation did not apply");
+  writeFileSync(mutantGate, source.replace(openAiLine, ""));
+  const controls = run(["bun", "run", mutantGate, "--controls-only"], clone);
+  const harness = run(["bun", "run", join(clone, "tools", "break-no-secrets-gate.ts")], clone);
+  put(clone, "synthetic-openai-token.txt", `sk-${"A".repeat(40)}\n`);
+  const leakRun = run(["bun", "run", mutantGate, "--tree-only", "--no-local-store"], clone);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector controls: ${controls.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector harness: ${harness.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector planted token: ${leakRun.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+} finally {
+  rmSync(scratch, { recursive: true, force: true });
+}

+
+  console.log("\nUNTESTED MUTATION");
+  const clone = join(scratch, "mutant-repo");
+  const cloneRun = run(["git", "clone", "-q", "--no-hardlinks", ROOT, clone], ROOT);
+  if (cloneRun.exit !== 0) throw new Error(cloneRun.err);
+  symlinkSync(join(ROOT, "node_modules"), join(clone, "node_modules"), "dir");
+  const mutantGate = join(clone, "tools", "check-no-secrets.ts");
+  const source = readFileSync(mutantGate, "utf8");
+  const openAiLine = '  { name: "OpenAI API key", re: /\\bsk-(?:proj-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{40,}/g },\n';
+  if (!source.includes(openAiLine)) throw new Error("OpenAI mutation did not apply");
+  writeFileSync(mutantGate, source.replace(openAiLine, ""));
+  const controls = run(["bun", "run", mutantGate, "--controls-only"], clone);
+  const harness = run(["bun", "run", join(clone, "tools", "break-no-secrets-gate.ts")], clone);
+  put(clone, "synthetic-openai-token.txt", `sk-${"A".repeat(40)}\n`);
+  const leakRun = run(["bun", "run", mutantGate, "--tree-only", "--no-local-store"], clone);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector controls: ${controls.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector harness: ${harness.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+  console.log(`deleted OpenAI detector planted token: ${leakRun.out.trimEnd().split("\n").at(-1)}`);
+} finally {
+  rmSync(scratch, { recursive: true, force: true });
+}
